Literally a "sprinkling vein" or "spring-vein," a metaphor for a source or wellspring of water, signifying the origin of spiritual life.</note>, from which this indwelling and inner working of **JESUS** in our hearts proceeds.

A common term in the 17th and 18th centuries for a small book or essay intended for popular devotion.</note> is therefore rightly called a **LIVING HEART-THEOLOGY**, because it looks only at the heart, so that **JESUS** might be recognized and found within the heart.

# The Publisher

*me in vain:* <note>original: "me te vergeefs" — completing the biblical quote from the previous page</note> but our low, worldly, and fleshly hearts are not capable, nor receptive, to preserve the rays of Jesus's love within them; there is constant sighing and complaining in the Soul, that they do not feel Jesus, nor see his face; but how can Jesus come into the Soul when it is filled with sins, and with unrighteousness; *he can make no dwelling with us.*

Ah! that while I write, my Soul might be more affected; O that I might write from experience <note>original: "ondervinding" — a key term in Dutch Second Reformation spirituality, referring to a personal, felt knowledge of God rather than just intellectual belief</note>, so that my words might not serve as a judgment for my soul.

This Heavenly and living Heart-Theology <term>Heart-Theology (Herts-Theologie): A style of divinity focused on the emotional and spiritual transformation of the inner person rather than just academic study</term> is in these latter days so unknown among men, that many are greatly amazed, as if not knowing what you say when you present to them this hidden Divinity <note>original: "Godtsgeleertheyd" — Theology or the study of God</note>, which nonetheless is so well known in God’s Word, that one should not be ignorant of it.

But it seems, because we do not comprehend these Divine and Heavenly things, *which are revealed only to the little children;* <note>A reference to Matthew 11:25: "thou hast hid these things from the wise and prudent, and hast revealed them unto babes."</note>

# The Publisher

Master they followed, with the testimony of God, who said: *This is my beloved Son, in whom I am well pleased; Hear Him.* <note>A reference to the Transfiguration of Jesus described in Matthew 17:5.</note>

Many might perhaps judge from this that I were an enemy of permissible Scholarship <term>Geleerdheden: formal education or academic learning</term> and Studies, and that I, like many in these days, would reject everything that could serve to guide one toward true wisdom and the knowledge of God; but I am not conscious of any such feeling. I judge that the wisdom of the learned Apostle to the Gentiles <note>The Apostle Paul</note>, who was instructed by Gamaliel <note>A celebrated doctor of Jewish law and teacher of Paul, mentioned in Acts 22:3</note>, can be beneficial to further proclaim the great virtues of God—provided that one, like him, considers that first-established learning (which rests in the intellect and not in the Soul) as nothing but foolishness when compared to the latter <note>referring to the spiritual wisdom mentioned previously</note>.

The most necessary part of his knowledge <term>wetenſchap: here meaning a branch of learning or a state of knowing</term>, and that which he sought after most, was to know Jesus Christ among his people, and him crucified: and this ought to be ours as well, for where the most is at stake, there also should the most be sought; *for the Father seeks such.* <note>A reference to John 4:23, "the Father seeks such to worship Him."</note>

You shall find in this little Treatise <term>Tractaatje: a small book or pamphlet, often on a religious subject</term> such

# To the Reader.

matters and states <term>geftalteniffen: internal conditions, dispositions, or spiritual appearances of the soul</term>, which belong only to those whom God has inwardly enlightened through His Spirit, so that they may understand those things which are granted to them by God. You shall find therein the complete mysteries of the heart, and that which the Lord Jesus works within it, when He has given the Soul a vision beyond that which the world neither knows nor sees.

Before this, two [books] of similar Emblems <term>Zinnebeelden: symbolic images accompanied by text, used to convey moral or spiritual truths</term> have already been published, namely the *Divine Meditations* <note>original: "Goddelyke Aandachten"</note>, or *Flaming Desires of a Penitent Soul* <note>original: "vlammende Begeerten eens Boetvaerdige Ziele"</note>. These were found to be not without fruit <note>meaning they were spiritually beneficial or successful</note>, and this has encouraged us all the more to bring this present work to light, especially as the first edition saw the light more than twenty years ago, and for several years now has no longer been available.

The other is a little Treatise <term>Tractaatje: a small book or pamphlet, typically on a religious or moral subject</term> named *Jesus and the Soul* <note>original: "Jefus en de Ziel"</note>, compiled by our good friend *Jan Luyken* <note>Jan Luyken (1649–1712) was a famous Dutch poet, illustrator, and engraver whose work defined the religious emblem genre in the Netherlands</note>, from whose spiritual and sweet-flowing rhymes we shall include several in this little book, fitting even these illustrations, which

The text highlights that this edition has been significantly expanded and concludes with a prayer for the reader's spiritual well-being.</meta>

# The Publisher to the Reader.

he made some years ago.
&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;We shall further tell the Reader that this Work is increased by more than half, and filled with new Verses, which stand before each Illustration <term>Verbeelding: an image, representation, or engraving</term>, and follow thereafter. Please use this Work then for the awakening of your Souls: oh that Jesus might take such a form in our hearts, that we would be able to live more to His honor. But God, who is powerful to do above all that we ask or think <note>A reference to the biblical passage in Ephesians 3:20</note>, give us His Spirit and His light to walk therein, while we here on earth must still sigh over our corrupted hearts; and may He fill the same with His Heavenly grace, until He shall come to bring us to that place where the tears and sighing of His companions in grace <term>gunftgenooten: those who share in God's favor or the "elect"; fellow partakers of divine grace</term> shall end, and eternal joy shall fill their Souls. Amen.

### Upon the
## L I V E N G
# HEART-THEOLOGY,
### Of
## CHRISTIAN HOBURG.

No rhyming thoughts tied to the worldly things,
Nor matter dedicated to fictional gods <note>original: "verdichte Goden"; a reference to classical Roman or Greek mythology, often contrasted with Christian truth in this period</note>,
Is this; no, Jesus himself comes here to kindly invite you
To Heavenly food, formerly purchased by him so dearly.

These **Emblems** <term>zinnebeelden: symbolic engravings or allegorical images intended to convey a moral or religious lesson</term> are so wondrously wrought
With soul-refreshment; yes, they are true **Heavenly breads**,
Wherein the Soul, as if entirely fled from the earthly,
Is ever refreshed by that fountain of true Heavenly moisture.

This glorious **Poetic Banquet**, flowed from HOBURG’S mind,
Is joyful for the spirit; here a soul, weary
From the heavy burden of sins, can find much comfort and refreshment.

This **HEART THEOLOGY**, which steadily satisfies the spirit,
Is peace for a Soul that submits itself in all things
To Christ’s will; these alone are God’s true friends.

->J. VERHEYDE, JUNIOR.<-

# The Heart of Jesus, and He our Heart.

## Plate I.

O Blessed Fountain of Salvation, who the salvation of mankind
->are,<-
For you my heart so flames, for you my soul cries out,
To you, and before your throne, where the blessed souls,
With faces covered <note>original: "Gedekt van aangefigt"; a reference to the angels or the redeemed who veil their faces in the presence of God's overwhelming glory, as described in Isaiah 6</note>, kneel down in reverence.
O Sun! O Heavenly Light! receive my flame of love,
Which from your Light took its first origin.
Receive a sighing heart, that in the midst of darkness,
Needs that spark, that Heavenly light, that
->luster,<-
That blessed foretaste of the Heavenly Paradise,
Which you give to your children, O that hidden food! <note>original: "verborgen fpys"; a reference to the "hidden manna" mentioned in Revelation 2:17, symbolizing internal spiritual nourishment known only to the believer</note>
Which no one ever knows, except those you first truly knew,
And who through faithful love constantly turned to you.
I lift my heart to you; O let your glow of love
Descend into my soul, then shall my sorrowful
->spirit,<-
Tossed up and down <note>original: "Geflingert op en neêr"; a metaphor for the soul's agitation and lack of peace in the worldly life</note>, find rest in you again,
And you, O dear Jesus! shall be my most beloved,
Then I shall return the loving heart that you gave me here below,
In full flame of love, back to you, Lord.

<meta>This page continues the first chapter following the introductory emblem of the heart. <header>Living</header>

## I.

# **Dialogue of the Soul.**

1. O faithful and loving Lord Jesus, how is your Heart then so exceedingly filled with fiery Love, that one sees the flame thereof breaking out so abundantly.

2. Ah! most wonderful eternal goodness, since it has pleased you to grant your Heart, burning in Love, to us as an eternal possession: grant me graciously that I may daily offer my Heart to you—out of a more indebted reciprocal love <note>original: "wederliefde"; the theological concept that the believer's love for God is a response to and a reflection of God's prior love for them</note>—as a sincere thank-offering.

3. O Jesus, the Soul’s only Treasure, see how full your blessed Heart is with Love, that one also sees the flames and rays thereof breaking out abundantly: Beloved! grant that a tiny spark of these your living flames of love might fall into my heart, and ignite the same at this very moment, such that it might become burning in Love and thankfulness toward you and your holy will.

4. Let this your fiery-burning glow of love, and heartfelt mercy toward us, O good Jesus, be my only treasure, my wealth and comfort, my refuge in need, my honor and glory, my Paradise and happiness, both in this time and for eternity.

<meta>catchword: 5. Yes</meta>

<summary>A devotional meditation in the form of a prayer, focusing on the "fiery love" emanating from the Heart of Jesus and the believer's desire to return that love as a thank-offering.</summary>
<keywords>Jesus, Heart of Jesus, Divine Love, thank-offering, spiritual treasure, reciprocal love, 17th-century devotion</keywords>

--- Page 29 ---
<meta>This page continues the "Dialogue of the Soul" from Chapter I. The text continues to expand on the imagery of the Heart of Jesus as a source of divine grace and the essential foundation for the believer's spiritual life.</meta>

# Heart-Theology.

5. Yes, good and faithful Lord Jesus, you eternal and only Love of my soul, this your loving and merciful Heart is the only spring-head <note>original: "Sprinkader"; a source or fountain-vein from which water flows</note>, from which all life-giving Light, Strength, Healing, and Salvation <note>original: "Saligheyt"; refers to both the state of being blessed and the state of being saved</note> springs forth toward us: this your Heart is the only and eternal foundation, upon which the house of my soul is built.

6. If this your loving Heart, O Savior <note>original: "Saligmaker"; literally "the one who makes blessed"</note> of souls, did not burn toward us in merciful Love from eternity to eternity, then there would be no more miserable creatures than we, both in time and in eternity.

7. Since you then grant us this your Jesus-Heart, you give us with the same everything that you are and have in time and in eternity: for this your Heart, breaking forth in a spiritual life-giving power, cleanses us, enlightens us, strengthens us, and makes us blessed.

8. Therefore is he truly blessed, who can say of you with truth: *My Friend Jesus is mine, and he also holds himself to me*: for were you not first mine, out of compassionate Love? And did you not first give your loving Jesus-Heart to me? How then could you ever come into my heart, and accomplish your Holy work in the same?

<sig>B 2</sig>
<meta>catchword: Sighing (Zucht)</meta>

<summary>A continuation of the prayerful dialogue where the soul recognizes Jesus's heart as the source of all spiritual life and the foundation of faith. It emphasizes that divine love precedes human response, as Jesus must first give His heart to the believer before they can receive Him.</summary>
<keywords>Heart of Jesus, Spring-head, Foundation of the soul, Salvation, Merciful love, Reciprocity, 17th-century Dutch devotion</keywords>

### I.
## *Soul-Dialogue.*

> 1. **O** faithful and loving Lord Jesus, how your Heart is so exceedingly filled with fiery Love, that one sees its flames breaking out from it abundantly.
>
> 2. Ah! most wondrous eternal goodness, since it has pleased you to grant us your Heart, burning in Love, as an eternal possession: grant me graciously that I may daily offer up my heart to you out of bounden return-love <note>original: "wederliefde"; the love a believer gives back to God in response to His grace</note> as a sincere thank-offering <note>original: "dankofferhande"; a ritual or spiritual sacrifice of gratitude</note>.
>
> 3. O Jesus, the soul's only Treasure, see how filled your blessed Heart is with Love, that one sees the flame and rays breaking out from it abundantly: Dear Lord! grant that a spark of these your living flames of Love may fall into my heart and ignite it at this very moment, so that it might burn in Love and gratitude toward you and your holy will.
>
> 4. Let this your fiery-burning glow of Love and heartfelt compassion toward us, O good Jesus, be my only treasure, my wealth and comfort, my refuge in need, my honor and glory, my Paradise and bliss <note>original: "geluksaligheyd"; refers to the state of being both perfectly happy and spiritually saved</note>, both now and for eternity.

<meta>This page continues the "Soul-Dialogue" from the previous section. # Heart-Theology.

5. Yes, good and faithful Lord Jesus, you eternal and only Love of my soul, this your loving and compassionate Heart is the only spring-head <note>original: "Sprinkader"; literally a source or fountainhead of a spring, implying a continuous flow</note> from which all life-giving Light, Strength, Salvation, and Bliss <note>original: "Saligheyt"; refers to the state of being spiritually saved and perfectly happy in God</note> springs forth toward us: this your Heart is the only and eternal Foundation upon which the House of my soul is built.

6. If this your loving Heart, O Savior of souls, did not burn toward us in compassionate Love from eternity to eternity, then there would be no more miserable creatures than we ourselves, both in time and in eternity.

7. Since you then grant us this your Jesus-Heart, you give us with it everything that you are and have in time and in eternity: for this your Heart, breaking out in a spiritual life-giving power, cleanses us, enlightens us, strengthens us, and makes us blessed.

8. Therefore he is indeed Blessed, who can say of you in truth: "My Friend Jesus is mine, and he also holds fast to me": for were you not first mine, out of compassionate Love? And did you not first give to me your loving Jesus-Heart? How could you ever come into my heart and accomplish your Holy work within it?

<meta>This page transitions from the theological definition of the Heart of Jesus as a source of life to a personal, poetic expression of the soul's intense longing for union with that source. The "Sigh" or "Longing" (Zucht) mentioned in the previous page's catchword begins here as a devotional poem.</meta>

Lively

<margin>I</margin>
## Longing of the Loving Soul.

My Jesus, oh! my love, my God, my life,
Now cries my spirit, by love strongly
->driven,<-
By love strongly hunted to a burning thirst.
Now cries my spirit with David from the breast: <note>A reference to King David, specifically the yearning expressed in Psalm 42: "As the deer pants for streams of water, so my soul pants for you, my God."</note>
My God! my love, in Heaven nor on earth,
Nothing delights me, but you alone my dear,
My food, my drink, O spirit of my spirit,
Who never began, but have been eternally;
<margin>I</margin>
You are all, and all that I desire,
My flesh and blood I wish to consume with joy
&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;In your love, oh! sweet Jesus oh!
My Bridegroom, how long must it yet wait,
Before my spirit unites with your Spirit,
Like a drop of water, which disappears
Lost in red wine, and becomes
The same color and taste as that into which it is poured. <note>This is a classic mystical metaphor for the soul’s union with the Divine, where the individual (the water) is completely transformed and absorbed by God (the wine) while maintaining its essence.</note>
<margin>I</margin>
&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;&nbsp;Oh if only I had the wings of a dove,
So that I might fly away from the earth,
<margin>I</margin>
I would fly so far, until I found rest
In my God, my true Fatherland.
Now my Spirit is driven strongly by love:
I long for death; for Christ is my life,
And dying is my precious gain! <note>A paraphrase of Philippians 1:21: "For to me, to live is Christ and to die is gain."</note>
Oh! only one, whom I alone love;
I have set you as a seal upon my heart, <note>Refencing Song of Solomon 8:6: "Place me like a seal over your heart, like a seal on your arm; for love is as strong as death."</note>
My Love, to lack you causes me pain:
My love, my God, my Jesus, all my good,
You are my treasure, my heart, and my mind
Lie hidden and buried deep within you,
I thirst for God! No creature can quench my thirst,
No created thing, however beautiful it may be;
I thirst for God, that living fountain.

# Heart-Theology. <note>In this context, "Heart-Theology" refers to a 17th-century mystical tradition that emphasized internal, emotional devotion over cold, intellectual doctrine.</note>

*The Lord demands the human heart in its entirety,*
*So give it, dear person, and you shall not regret it,*
*And keep not for yourself even the smallest part,*
*Thus shall you eternally behold His face.*

*You have, O Lord, desired no sacrifice*
*Of cattle or beast, but clearly taught*
*That we should give our heart, the foundation of our life,*
*Into Your will and your bonds;*
*Therefore take, O Lord, take all our hearts,*
*And do with them whatever shall please You.*

### Longing of the Soul. <note>The original Dutch term "Suchtinge" literally means "Sighing," referring to a form of prayer that is felt as a deep, wordless breath or yearning of the spirit.</note>

*Give me, out of pure favor, Your generous and loving heart,*
*So that through its help, I may prepare mine according to Your wishes*
*For Your praise, and free it from its pain.*

# Here Jesus protects the Heart.

## II. Figure. <note>In 17th-century devotional books, a "Figure" refers to an emblematic illustration that accompanied the poem, designed to be meditated upon.</note>

O Strong Rock! O foundation stone full of trust,
You are a Shield for the embattled heart.
When it sighs in the deepest distress,
It is quickly helped by You, O Lord.
My poor Heart, which is as if bound fast,
Still to that which You, my *Life*, <note>Refers to Christ as the source of eternal life.</note> hate,
What shall I do but flee to Your wounds, <note>A common image in historical devotion where the wounds of the crucified Christ are seen as a literal shelter or "cleft in the rock" where the soul can hide from evil.</note>
So that You might guard it from all of Satan's evil:
O dear Jesus! when I wish to send my Heart,
Towards You, ah, what is it that meets me:
See there the flesh, the world? what misery!
Satan comes, and shows me all his goods.
But You who are a King of the Soul,
Whom You have bought through Your own blood,
Before whom everything that exists must kneel,
And fall before Your Majesty at Your feet.
You shall protect and preserve my Heart,
You shall be my Shield and strength, O Lord!
You shall surely rescue my Heart,
From all kinds of dangers; for this is Your highest Honor!
If You were not my Screen, my Shield, and Sun,
Ah! what would I not constantly fall into,
To what would the <term>flesh</term> <note>In historical theology, "the flesh" (vleesch) refers to human nature's inclination toward sin and worldly desires.</note> not be able to lead me,
As it already happens, O Lord, all too often.

->Psalm 84. verse 6.<- <note>In modern English Bibles, this is usually Psalm 84:5.</note>

> *Blessed is the man whose strength is in You, in whose Heart are the paved ways.* <note>The "paved ways" or "highways" refer to the spiritual paths leading to God's presence.</note>

--- Page 36 ---
<meta>This page continues the sequence of meditations on the heart from Figure II. Having established that Jesus protects the heart from the external assaults of the world and the devil, this "Soul-Conversation" provides a first-person prayer or dialogue (soliloquy) where the believer laments the constant spiritual warfare and expresses gratitude for Christ’s guidance in recognizing these traps.</meta>

Living

## II.

### Soul-Conversation.

1. See now, O good Jesus, how the world on one side and the enemy <note>Typically referring to the devil or Satan.</note> on the other side combat my heart; I believe, O merciful Lord, that no city may be so hard besieged as this heart of mine by these enemies.
2. My heart is small, but these enemies of mine are great; great they are in power, but even greater in cunning; See how many snares <note>original: "strikken"—a common 17th-century metaphor for temptations or spiritual traps.</note> they set for me: snares in eating, snares in drinking, in speaking, in silence, in waking, in sleeping; Ah Lord! who can know and escape them all?
3. O good and faithful Lord Jesus, how my soul must praise You for that great grace, that You have shown me some of these snares of my enemies: ah, grant me further this mercy, that I may flee these same snares which You have shown me in Your light, through Your strength.
4. Is it any wonder, O my Lord Jesus, that people are so easily misled, since everywhere and in all things so many snares are set for them, and that the heart is surrounded by so many enemies, where yet, alas! <note>original: "ach lacy", an archaic Dutch exclamation of grief or regret.</note> so little heed is paid to it.

<meta>catchword: 5. Sou-</meta>

<summary>In this personal prayer, the soul laments the constant spiritual attacks from the world and the devil, comparing the heart to a besieged city surrounded by hidden traps in everyday activities.</summary>
<keywords>spiritual warfare, snares, temptation, vigilance, Jesus as protector, heart meditation, 17th-century devotion</keywords>

6. For see, O faithful Lord Jesus, how these enemies with their snares are so powerful within me: wherever they are, O Lord, there You are also with Your victorious help. ## Sigh of the Soul.

Oh, that my heart were cleansed from sins,
Through Jesus’ precious blood,
So that instead of the joy that should be found in my Soul,
I must now mourn.
Alas, my God! my Jesus, my beloved,
Oh, be not far from me:
Satan’s most loyal and dearest friends <note>A reference to worldly temptations or sinful inclinations that "befriend" the soul to its own detriment.</note>
I have here at my side,
Who seek to pull the heart away from You, Lord,
Through a thousand wicked ways.
Oh, assist me, O Lord, I am distressed, <note>original: "verlegen" — in this context, meaning to be at a loss, troubled, or in a state of spiritual anxiety.</note>
Or else I shall sink down.
My Soul sighs for You, O light and life,
My comfort and only good!
Please constantly give strength to my spirit,
O Jesus! most sweet,
When You make Your power felt in the soul,
Then flesh and blood <note>A biblical term (Matthew 16:17) referring here to human nature and carnal desires as opposed to the spirit.</note> retreat,
And cannot toss and turn as is its will,
Because You, Lord, protect it.

*The World, Satan, and the Flesh, they seek with deceit*
*To constantly bind all our hearts alike:*
*O Jesus, dear Lord, do deliver our souls,*
*So that our enemies do not devour them eternally.*

<vocab>Sigh of the Soul, Jesus, Satan, World, Flesh, Soul, sins, blood, protect, devour</vocab>

<keywords>purification, spiritual warfare, precious blood, mourning, temptation, divine strength, the three enemies</keywords>

# Heart-Theology.

*The world, cunning, seeks with deceit*
*To keep our hearts here even now;*
*She flatters and pleads, and ever strives*
*To hold us fast within her power:*
*The Devil does not want to let us*
*Depart from his dominion unharmed,*
*He hooks his sting <note>original: "angel" — can mean a sting, a fish-hook, or a barb; here it suggests the painful, piercing grip of sin or temptation.</note> into our heart,*
*And causes us much anguish and pain;*
*And even our own flesh with vile desires,*
*Opposes us in our time of need.*
*If you wish to escape all these snares,*
*Call upon Jesus as your helper.*

# Heart-Theology.

The *World*, cunning, <note>original: "loos" — meaning sly or wily</note> seeks with deceit
To keep our *heart* still here below;
It flatters and begs, and always strives
To hold us fast within its power:
The *Devil* will not leave us unmolested
Or let us go from out of his grip,
He hooks his barb <note>original: "angel" — referring to a fishhook or a sting, symbolizing how temptation catches the soul</note> into our *heart*,
And causes us much *anguish* and *pain*;
And even our own flesh with vile desires,
Opposes us in times of need.
If you wish to escape all these snares,
Call upon *Jesus* as your helper.

# Here Jesus knocks at the Heart.

## III. Figure. <note>original: "Figuur" — this likely refers to an engraved illustration or "emblem" that accompanied the text in the original printed book.</note>

O Faithful Shepherd of your Sheep,
O joy, O salvation, and Bliss,
How is my Heart disposed within,
While you are there knocking at it:
I hardly heard your touch,
The silence of the rest of sin
I had already begun to taste,
And was almost fast asleep.
But you who are faithful in your deeds,
And will never leave the sighing mind
To lie in evil,
If only a Soul falls at your feet.
I have often, against your heartfelt knocking,
When you were there before the door,
Stopped up my ears and Heart,
Even though you called in mourning:
Open up my love, you my friend, <note>original: "vrindinne" — the feminine form of friend, used here because the Soul (de Ziel) is traditionally personified as female in devotional literature.</note>
And let me in to you,
You are the one whom I contemplate,
And love in the highest:
When the radiance of your Divine being
Pierced through the cracks
Of the Heart, full of darkness and fears,
Still she would not step forward.
Carelessness lay in the heart,
Locked fast and strong,
Even though you knocked, and were full of sorrow,
My Heart resembled a tombstone. <note>original: "Serk" — a heavy stone slab used for a grave, signifying that the heart was cold and spiritually dead.</note>
Therefore O Jesus, press inside,
And you open it, Lord,
For you are the one who can overcome,
For to you belongs the Honor.

->Song of Solomon 5: 2.<-

> *I slept, but my Heart waked; the voice of my Beloved that knocked, was etc.*

### III.

# Soul-Dialogue. <note>original: "Zielen-Geſprek" — a meditative genre where the inner soul speaks directly to God, often used to help readers personalize their faith.</note>

1. O dear and faithful Lord **Jesus**, how patiently you stand before the door of my Heart, and how zealously you knock upon the same through all kinds of outward and inward good stirrings and urgings toward the forsaking of evil, and toward the choosing of the good, so often rebuking me in my <term>Conscience</term> <note>original: "Conſcientie" — the inner sense of right and wrong, seen here as the place where God's voice is heard.</note>.

2. Oh! how I have let you, my dearest and most faithful Friend, <unclear>so</unclear> long a time wait with long-suffering, and <unclear>so</unclear> many years and days knock in vain: and how is it even possible that you, O eternal goodness, have waited so long for my <term>conversion</term> <note>original: "bekeeringe" — a turning of the soul back toward God.</note>.

3. And just as I must wonder exceedingly at this your unspeakable long-suffering patience, bearing with me for such a long time, having compassion for me, and not ceasing to knock at the door of my heart: so must I wonder much more at my own greatest impertinence, rudeness, and neglect, that I have let you, such a dear and worthy **Guest**, stand outside so unfriendlily and knock in vain.

<summary>This page presents an intimate prayer or "Soul-Dialogue" where the speaker laments their past neglect of Christ's persistent and patient calls to the heart. It emphasizes the contrast between divine long-suffering and human stubbornness using the metaphor of a guest waiting at a door.</summary>

<keywords>Soul-Dialogue, Jesus knocking, heart's door, conscience, conversion, patience, divine guest, repentance</keywords>

The text maintains the intimate, second-person address to Jesus characteristic of 17th-century Dutch devotional literature.</meta>

->Herts-Theologie.<-

...stand outside so unfriendlily and knock in vain,] and that I still so often give so little heed to your knocking within me.

4. O good **Jesus**, forgive me this my sluggishness <note>original: "traagheyd" — also meaning sloth or spiritual apathy.</note> and ungratefulness; and let the **Love**, through which you have been moved to knock at my door for so long a time, so rule my **Heart**, that I may open to you as you knock, and with a hungry desire for the working of your **grace**, may let you into my heart.

5. Now then, my **Soul**, stand up quickly, without any delay: do you not hear how your Friend **Jesus** knocks? Do not neglect this great **grace** any longer, but open up to him quickly, <unclear>that he</unclear> <note>The words "dat hy" (that he) are partially obscured by a dark brown stain in the manuscript.</note> may enter into you, and unite himself with you in grace, temporally and eternally, and hold his **Supper** <term>Avondmaal</term> <note>original: "Avondmaal" — while literally "evening meal," in this context it refers to the mystical "Lord’s Supper" or spiritual communion, referencing Revelation 3:20: "I will come in to him and eat with him."</note> in you.

# Lively <note>original: "Levendige" — likely part of a larger title, "Lively [or Vivid] Heart-Theology."</note>

<margin>1</margin>
See here, O Human <note>original: "Menſch" — often used in a universal sense to address all of humanity.</note>, here comes the Man,
Who can deliver us from evil;
He knocks and listens at your door,
And waits there patiently <note>original: "onverdrietig" — literally "un-sorrowfully," meaning without becoming annoyed or giving up despite the wait.</note> before it.

->**Heart-Theology**<-

My God, I have indeed heard you,
Why then do you linger at the gate
Of a worm <note>A common 17th-century metaphor for human insignificance and unworthiness in the presence of the Divine.</note>, who can give you nothing,
So entirely long-suffering, night and day?
O Love! that from the Father’s heart
Was born from eternity:
My Soul would so gladly let you in;
But good Lord, my **Household** <term>Huisgezin</term> <note>original: "Huiſgeſin" — while literally meaning "family," in this mystical context it refers to the unruly passions, distractions, and sins that "dwell" within the human heart and resist spiritual progress.</note>,
A stinking breeding-nest of Hell,
Resists my good will too fiercely;
If I slide the bolt of the door,
They slide it back in front again:
I am distressed <note>original: "benauwt" — conveys a sense of being trapped, anxious, or spiritually suffocated.</note>, my Lord, my God,
You must help me; break the lock,
And wrench the door open with force,
And see how I am situated:
Come in to me, and make me pure,
And let me be a dwelling for you.

## *Petit Bourdeaux.* <note>original: "Petit Bourdeaux" — This refers to a popular 17th-century French melody. It was common in songbooks of this era to indicate a well-known tune so the reader could sing the lyrics.</note>

->I.<-

See, He who created Heaven and Earth,
Stands here at the door, my Soul,
He is the Creator, who gave you birth;
You are the creature: bow and kneel:
See, He is the **Potter** <term>Pottebakker</term>,
And you are an earthen vessel <note>A biblical metaphor (from Jeremiah and Romans) highlighting human fragility and God's sovereign craftsmanship.</note>.
Whatever you are, you are then indebted
To Him from whom you received everything.

->2.<-

Turn yourself then away from all things,
And also away from your own self:
God from whom we received everything,
Who gave us His entire self:
To You be everything dedicated,
Whether it be my highest **blessedness** <term>zaligheyd</term>,
If only I may please You,
You who are my **origin** <term>oorſpronk</term>.

->3.<-

But what then shall I yet give?
I have fallen so deep into debt,
Creator of my first life,
Before You even fashioned me,
Through Your suffering, through Your death,
Where would I find anything [to give]? Where?
You are God, and I am human,
Eternally Your **debtor** <term>ſchuldenaar</term>.

# THOMAS à KEMPIS <note>Thomas à Kempis (c. 1380–1471) was a German-Dutch canon regular and the author of *The Imitation of Christ*, one of the most influential Christian devotional books.</note>

->*says:*<-

THE Voice of Christ is a voice sweet to hear, and blessed for everyone who is obedient to it. Love is a rejoicing of the spirit, a Paradise of Souls; it shuts out the world, conquers the Devil, closes Hell, and opens Heaven. Christ and the love of the world are opposites; they have nothing in common, nor can they dwell together. The love of Christ is Elijah’s chariot <note>A reference to 2 Kings 2:11, where the prophet Elijah is taken to heaven in a chariot of fire; here it symbolizes the soul's ascent through divine love.</note>, ascending toward Heaven. The love of the world is the Devil’s chariot, pulling toward Hell. Self-love is one’s own wounding; the forsaking of the world is the finding of Heaven. It is pleasant and good to hear, but more praiseworthy to practice in deed. The improvement of one’s life is the very best **Sermon** <term>Predikatie</term>. The fruits of a good sermon are to abstain from sins and to advance in virtues. The fruit of a sanctified prayer is to unite oneself with God through the power of the **Holy Spirit** <term>H. Geest</term>.

->*O most beautiful Love! how sweet*<-
->*Is your voice within my spirit;*<-
->*It surpasses all lutes and strings,*<-
->*When you, God’s beloved Son,*<-
->*Will take up your dwelling in my heart,*<-
->*And there declare yourself King.*<-

<vocab>Thomas à Kempis, Christ, Devil, Heaven, Hell, Elijah’s chariot, Sermon, Holy Spirit</vocab>

<summary>This page features a collection of spiritual maxims attributed to Thomas à Kempis, emphasizing that true "Heart-Theology" consists of practicing virtues rather than just hearing sermons. It concludes with a short, celebratory verse welcoming Christ into the heart as King.</summary>
<keywords>Thomas à Kempis, Divine Love, asceticism, Elijah's chariot, spiritual practice, internal kingship, Holy Spirit</keywords>

### Yearning of the Soul. <note>The original Dutch "Suchtinge" literally means "sighing," but in a 17th-century devotional context, it refers to the deep, wordless longing or yearning of the soul in prayer.</note>

> *O Jesus, faithful friend, how often have you*
> *Faithfully knocked at my heart's door;*
> *Ah! break in yourself, deliver my Soul from death,*
> *And dwell there continually, and stay no longer outside.*

> *When you, dear God, come knocking at our heart,*
> *Then please open the door, and come inside yourself;*
> *The bolt that was held there by sin,*
> *Take away, for without you, we cannot begin.*

> *When you, O good Lord, shall have opened*
> *The door of my heart, and have come inside,*
> *And you shall fill it with yourself from now on,*
> *Then from that heart shall spring streams of Love.*

# Jesus searches the Heart here.
## IV. Figure.

O Faithful Jesus! good Lord!
How you now come to search my heart,
Both below and above again,
Through all the most hidden and deepest corners.
What do you find there, O Friend of Souls!
But scorpions and snakes,
Everything that you do not love,
And that comes to oppress the Heart. <note>The original Dutch "prangen" implies a painful squeezing or weighing down by sin.</note>
O, if you with your blessed light,
Had never entered into my Heart,
And if your friendly countenance
Had not looked upon me in peace;
I would never have noticed that nest of sin,
Full of malice, and of a thousand evils,
Nor known at the last,
With whom my soul should take counsel,
Now you come yourself, O Bridegroom,
To find that which was lost,
And stir up the heart's malice,
And love your friends with tender love.
O people of the world, take note,
If you saw the malice and the lusts,
Which are imprisoned <note>The Dutch "gekerkt" literally means "churched" but here functions as "imprisoned" or "cloistered" within the heart's depths.</note> in your heart,
You would not rest for a single moment,
Until that Friend of Souls [had] searched
Your Heart; and you had found light,
By which you could see that which pains you,
And that which would wound you unto death.

# Living Heart-Theology.

### Soul-Dialogue.

1. O Only Goodness, source of life, searcher of hearts and reins <note>The "reins" (nieren) refers to the kidneys, which in seventeenth-century biblical language represented the seat of one's most secret emotions and desires.</note>, you searching through my unclean Heart, what ravenous, unclean beasts shall you find therein; which have come forth from the poisonous seed of the old serpent <note>A reference to the biblical serpent in Eden, symbolizing the origin of sin.</note>.

2. O my Soul! do give over your Heart to this your Jesus, Savior and only Preserver, so that he may shine through the same with the light of his grace, and search it to the deepest ground, discover to you all evil deceitful uncleanness, give you a hatred against it, and graciously deliver you from it.

3. Think not, O Soul! that you, without his gracious illumination and diligent examination of yourself, would ever come to recognize the abyss of deceitfulness, perversity, and uncleanness of your corrupt heart: ah no, my Soul, the Creator and Redeemer of your heart, can and must fathom the same alone, he

# Heart-Theology. <note>original: "Herts-Theologie"</note>

4. Therefore, O most faithful Lord Jesus <note>original: "aldergetrouwste Heere Jesu"</note>, see, from this hour onward, I give over my Heart <note>original: "Herte"</note> to you; you, you, O Lord! who have created and mercifully redeemed it, fathom it also, search it; for it is impossible for me to do so myself. Show me my own self, and my evil condition, so that I may become an abomination in my own eyes, and thus begin to hate and forsake myself, and may obtain a heartfelt longing for you, my only Savior <note>original: "Heyland"</note>, and for the new creature <note>The "new creature" (nieuwe Creature) refers to the spiritual transformation of the believer, a concept rooted in 2 Corinthians 5:17: "if any man be in Christ, he is a new creature."</note>, which you shall create in me through your grace.

# Living <note>original: "Levendige"; likely part of a running header such as "Living Faith" or "Living Hope."</note>

O Lord, when the first light of the Spirit,
Proceeding from your grace, has enlightened us,
What sinful rabble, what evil scum,
Does every man find within his own home: <note>The "home" refers here to the internal state of the individual's soul or heart.</note>
He surely hopes for salvation, but in the place
Of salvation, there is nothing but every kind of evil.
What shame comes over him, what distress,
When his very foundation <note>original: "grond" — referring to the deepest essence or "ground" of the soul, a common mystical and theological concept.</note> is thus laid bare?
To whom you do not offer your help,
He must perish in this sorrow.

When Christ moves into the Heart,
And with his light uncovers the foundation,
Then it is truly found to be so:
Oh me! what sins are nesting here!
It swarms there dreadfully, all through each other;
Everything that hides there becomes manifest,
Where it lurks and lies in wait:
Who can clear out this filthy den? <note>original: "Byaerd" — an archaic Dutch term for a place of filth, a pigsty, or a scene of great disorder.</note>
Who else but Jesus, the long-promised one,
Who treads upon the head of the Serpent. <note>A reference to Genesis 3:15, the "First Gospel," where it is prophesied that the seed of the woman would crush the head of the serpent.</note>

Drive out all evil beasts, <note>Metaphorical representation of sins or demonic influences lurking in the soul.</note>
So that praise and peace may be heard,

Brought forth blessedly
Through your immeasurable power.

->2.<-

Let all storms finally vanish;
Command the wind, and say that it must lie down,
Speak to the north wind, "turn back,"
Then soon there shall be a great stillness: <note>A reference to Christ calming the storm on the Sea of Galilee (Mark 4:39).</note>

That they may shine here and there upon the earth;
I am a vain and empty earth, <note>Refers to the soul as uncultivated soil waiting for divine intervention.</note>

Drench my heart with Heavenly dew;
Send down your graces; reach down to me
The water of devotion, <note>original: "aandagtigheden"; refers to the practices and state of prayerful mindfulness and spiritual focus.</note>
So that my soil might be watered therewith,
So that it grows and blooms joyfully,
Through all the blessing that flows from above,
So that it may bear an abundance of fruit
To your honor and good pleasure,
Most high Lord!

Header: Living

### Further Reflection.

We are blessed when the Lord Jesus comes to search our heart, when He shines within with the light of His Holy Spirit; then that which we previously overlooked and had not paid such close attention to is made manifest. There is nothing that sets itself more against the light of God than our own flesh, our sinful lusts, and our evil and wicked heart. These enemies are capable (unless Jesus comes to our aid) of darkening His light and friendly countenance, and of bringing us into a state that is so sad and miserable that few realize it.

Oh! that we might then let Jesus search and re-search our hearts, so that He might find what we, through our blindness, cannot see or find; it shall be better for our soul. For when He has found the unrighteousness in our heart, then He shall come with the broom of grace <note>A vivid metaphor common in 17th-century Pietist literature, suggesting a thorough, active purification of the inner self.</note> and cleanse it of all that which wickedly rests within, so that it may no longer rule over us nor exercise further power, according to the Word, which says that He came into the world to destroy the works of Satan. <note>A reference to 1 John 3:8: "The reason the Son of God appeared was to destroy the devil's work."</note>

<meta>catchword: Such-</meta>

<summary>This page reflects on the necessity of allowing Jesus and the Holy Spirit to search the "wicked heart" to reveal hidden sins. It emphasizes that Christ uses the "broom of grace" to cleanse the soul and destroy the works of the devil within the believer.</summary>
<keywords>heart-searching, Holy Spirit, sinful lusts, broom of grace, spiritual cleansing, Satan's works, internal darkness</keywords>

Heart-Theology <note>original: "Herts-Theologie." A term used in 17th-century Pietist movements to describe a focus on the internal spiritual life and the "circumcision of the heart" rather than just external dogma.</note>.

## Sighs of the Soul <note>original: "Zuchtinge der Ziele." In devotional literature of this period, a "sigh" refers to a short, intense prayer or an upward longing of the spirit toward God.</note>.

*Since one cannot truly deny oneself,*
*Unless one first knows their own wickedness!*
*So please, O Lord, make my sin known to me,*
*Send the light of your Spirit into my heart for this.*

*See, Christ enlightens every person through His radiance,*
*And reveals the pit—the heart full of sins,*
*And if He Himself does not establish life therein,*
*Then the person is completely swallowed by the fear of death.*

*Truly blessed is that heart where Jesus comes to dwell,*
*When He first cleanses it to be His home,*
*What streams of virtue shall spring from it,*
*When Jesus frees it from the evil and hellish rabble* <note>original: "gespuys." A derogatory term meaning "scum" or "rabble," here personifying sins or demonic influences as a disorderly mob occupying the heart.</note>.

->JOHN 1 vs. 9.<-

This was the true light, which enlightens every person coming into the world.

->JOHN 8 vs. 12.<-

I am the light of the world: he who follows me shall not walk in darkness, but shall have the light of life.

Lively <note>This is part of the running header, likely from the full title of the work, *Levendige Herts-Theologie* (Lively Heart-Theology).</note>

### Further Reflection.

**B**lessed are we when the Lord Jesus comes to examine our hearts; when He shines light therein with the light of His Holy Spirit, then that which we had previously overlooked, and had not so closely noted, becomes manifest within. There is nothing that sets itself more against the light of God than our own flesh, our sinful lusts, and our evil and wicked heart. These enemies are capable (unless Jesus comes to our aid) of darkening His light and His friendly countenance, and of bringing us into a state that is so sad and miserable that few know of it.

Ah! that we might then let Jesus search and re-search our hearts, so that He might find that which we, through our blindness, can neither see nor find; it shall be better for our souls. For when He has found the iniquity in our heart, then He shall come with the broom of grace <note>original: "befem der genade." A vivid domestic metaphor common in 17th-century Dutch devotional writing, suggesting a thorough, vigorous sweeping of the "inner house" of the soul to remove the dust of sin.</note> and cleanse the same of all that which rests evilly within it, so that it may no longer rule over us, nor exercise more power, according to the word which says that He came into the world to break the works of Satan <note>A reference to 1 John 3:8: "The reason the Son of God appeared was to destroy the devil's work."</note>.

It emphasizes the "broom of grace" as a means of purifying the soul from the internal dominion of sin and the works of Satan.</summary>

## Sighs of the Soul. <note>original: "Zuchtinge der Ziele." In 17th-century devotional literature, a "sigh" was a short, rhythmic prayer or poetic aspiration meant to be breathed out by the believer in a moment of spiritual intensity.</note>

Unless one first recognizes one's own wickedness!
Therefore, O Lord, please make my sin manifest to me,
And send the light of Your Spirit into my heart for this purpose.

Behold, Christ enlightens every person through His radiance,

Lively <note>This header is likely part of the title "Levendige Herts-Theologie" (Lively Heart-Theology).</note>

## Further Reflection.

Happy are we when the Lord Jesus comes to examine our heart, when He shines therein with the light of His Holy Spirit; for then that which we had previously overlooked, and had not so closely observed, is made manifest. There is nothing that sets itself more against the light of God than our own flesh, our sinful lusts, and our evil and wicked heart. These enemies are capable (unless Jesus comes to our aid) of darkening His light and His friendly countenance, and of bringing us into a condition that is so mournful and miserable that few are even aware of it.

Oh! That we might then allow Jesus to search and search through our hearts again, so that He might find what we, through our blindness, can neither see nor find, and it shall be better for our souls; for when He has found the iniquity in our heart, then He shall come with the broom of grace <note>original: "befem der genade." A common metaphor in 17th-century Dutch devotional writing, suggesting a vigorous, divine house-cleaning of the soul.</note> and purify the same from all that wickedly rests within it, so that it may no longer rule over us, nor exercise any more power, according to the word which says that He came into the world to destroy the works of Satan.

Living
<page-num>44</page-num>

## V. Figure.

Root out, root out, O Friend of Souls,
With Your broom of grace,
All that You find wickedly in me,
And that can harm my soul in any way:
Oh, drive out this hellish rabble <note>original: "Hels gespuys." This refers to the various demons or personified sins often depicted in these emblem books as monsters being chased out of the heart.</note>,
Which You, O Jesus! have found,
And bring my soul home to You,
Heal me from the slightest sins,
So that the heart may be for You alone,
My Bridegroom and my King!
And do not rest, until it is pure,
And that it serves as a dwelling for You.
Then my heart shall be emptied,
Of that which kept You outside,
When You have swept it clean,
Of all that vile monster of Hell <note>original: "snoô gedrogt der Hellen." Likely referring to the collective presence of sin and demonic influence inhabiting the unregenerate heart.</note>.
You are that great God and Lord,
Who is higher than all high ones;

To help us in our suffering.

### V.

### Dialogue of the Soul.

1. O how blessed and happy is that soul—blessed indeed!—from whose heart You sweep away <note>original: "uytvaeget"; this word specifically connects to the image of the broom used by Jesus in the previous emblem</note> all impurity, cleansing it through Your unspeakable grace. Therefore, O Eternal Love, deliver me graciously from all those impurities which You have also shown me out of Your grace.

2. For just as little, O Jesus, as I could see my own impurity <term>onreynigheyt</term> <note>meaning spiritual filth or sinfulness</note> of my own accord, much less can I deliver myself from it without Your help and grace.

3. Is it not an exceedingly great, even a twofold love, O loving Lord, that You not only reveal and point out the impurity of my heart and its deepest deceptions <term>bedriegerye</term> in Your Light, but that You also cleanse that same heart through Your great grace?

4. For truly, O Lord, my power is much too weak, and all instruments and means of this world are much too meager, my

5. What are you doing then, my soul? For what do you delay? ## VI. Figure.
### Here the Heart of JESUS was further cleansed.

The heart is deep, who can fathom it?
None but God who created it,
O Lord! do search all around,
Extract the deeply hidden sins,
And sanctify with Your precious blood,
The House in which You must dwell,
O Blood! of higher value,
Than Heaven and Earth.

---

Seek, Jesus, and bring to the light,
What may yet be secretly lurking,
That no venom, nor evil <term>rabble</term> <note>original: "gespuys"; refers to a crowd of low-life or wicked people, used here as a metaphor for demonic influences or persistent sinful impulses.</note>
Remains any longer in Your House;
For behold, I have into Your power
Exposed my Soul and body,
So cleanse everything, smallest and greatest,
Through the water of Your Holy Spirit.

### VI.
## Soul-Dialogue.

1. O Most Beautiful and loving Jesus, You who have driven out Satan with his monster <note>original: "gedrogt"; referring to the demonic presence or the "beast" of sin depicted in the previous illustration.</note> from my heart, and now enter my Soul further, to cleanse my heart of my hidden deviations. <note>original: "afdwalingen"; these are the secret errors or straying of the spirit that aren't always obvious to the sinner.</note> Ah! how good You are, most faithful and loving Friend of the Soul; cleanse and purify as much as it pleases You, until my heart may be entirely pure.

2. O Good Jesus! what secret and hidden deviations still remain, which we cannot find out, nor even grasp through our darkness; but You who are the eternal and blessed light (and before whose eyes nothing is hidden), see everything naked and open before You.

3. O Lord Jesus! we do not yet know even the very least of our sins; our eyes are dark, and we often neither can nor want to see our own evil; ah! what a great darkness and ignorance this is.

4. Should we not beseech You night and day, until You come to [cleanse] our heart entirely and

<header>Lively</header> <note>original: "Levendige"; this is likely a fragment of a title such as "Lively Depictions" or "Vivid Images," a common phrasing in 17th-century emblem books meant to bring spiritual truths to life.</note>

### VI. Figure. <note>In this context, a "Figure" refers to a spiritual emblem—a combination of an image (implied) and a poetic meditation used for moral instruction.</note>

How good You are, O dearest Lord!
That such a Soul can experience,
Whom You draw through Your tender love,
And treat as Your beloved;
When You, O Friend of Souls! <note>A popular title for Jesus in Dutch and German Pietist literature, emphasizing an intimate, mystical union between the believer and Christ.</note>
Still come to search the Heart most closely,
It is then that You find much wickedness!
Which hid itself in the corners.
So Jesus, most beautiful Lord,
Do not let any sin rest there,
Ah, sweep them all away together,
Which still troubled my heart;
Then shall that Royal Throne,
Be cleansed of the wicked crowds, <note>The "wicked crowds" refers to the many sins or worldly distractions that formerly occupied the heart, which is here envisioned as a throne meant only for God.</note>
Then You could take Your dwelling there,
And enter into my Heart,
With all Your love and Your Cross,
With all Your sorrow, and Your bonds,
For behold, it is Your own House,
Purified by You, by Your own hands.

In this period, a "cleansed conscience" was the goal of the "Second Reformation" (Nadere Reformatie) in the Netherlands.</note>, and the body being washed with pure water.*

| :--- | :--- |
| Figure | An emblem or symbolic illustration. |
| Soul | The spiritual essence of the person. |
| Heart | The center of the will and emotions in "Heart-Theology." |
| Jesus | The central figure of the believer's affection. |
| Cross | Symbols of Christ's suffering and the believer's imitation of Him. |
| Cleansed | The process of spiritual purification or sanctification. |
| Conscience | The inner witness of one's moral state before God. |

<header>Heart Theology.</header>

## Further Reflection.

When a believing Soul’s heart is purified by the Lord Jesus, she <note>In 17th-century Dutch, the "Soul" (Ziele) is grammatically feminine and often personified as a bride or female figure seeking her divine Groom.</note> finds a great spaciousness within herself. Then the Soul is filled with shouting and joy, where before there was nothing but sorrow, lamenting, and moaning over all the sins that still lived within her.

> Therefore, O best and kindest Jesus, do cleanse my heart thoroughly; let Satan take no more rest there; let my heart henceforth be a Temple for You, a House for Your dwelling, just as You have spoken: *My House shall be called a House of prayer.* <note>A reference to Jesus’ words in Matthew 21:13 regarding the sanctity of the Temple.</note>

May I also take care, more and more, to guard my Soul, which You have once purified, so that I do not return again, like a dog to its vomit. <note>A stark biblical metaphor for a person returning to their former sins, found in Proverbs 26:11 and 2 Peter 2:22.</note>

> O Lord Jesus! let Your grace support me constantly, so that I am not led astray by my desires and sinful heart, and thereby provoke You to anger.

-> 1 JOHN 5: 18. <-
<note>The original Dutch text cites this as 2 John 5:18, but the verse provided is actually from the first epistle of John.</note>

> *We know that everyone who is born of God does not sin, but he who is born of God guards himself, and the evil one does not touch him.*

<keywords>purification, Temple of the heart, spiritual joy, 1 John 5:18, guarding the soul, Pietism, repentance</keywords>

### VII.

## *Dialogue of the Soul.*

1. Look and behold this great wonder of God’s incomprehensible Love, who gave His only Son for this purpose: to cleanse your penitent heart <note>The "penitent heart" refers to a soul that is sincerely sorry for its sins and seeks spiritual renewal.</note> of all its sins, and to wash it in the Fountain of His Love, which springs from His holy wounds.

2. O good Jesus, how parched is my heart when You do not appear within it, to refresh it with the true Water of Life <term>Water of Life</term><note>A common biblical metaphor (John 4:14) for the spiritual grace that provides eternal life and satisfaction to the soul.</note>.

3. Therefore You Yourself, O faithful <term>Savior</term><note>original: "Saligmaker"; literally "one who makes blessed" or "bringer of salvation."</note>, must descend into my naturally unclean Heart, and cleanse me there through Your blood; and with the water of Your grace, wash my Soul of her sins, so that I may become white as snow before the eyes of Your Heavenly Father.

4. Do not be slow or negligent, then, O my soul, but run quickly to meet this Heavenly Fountain of Life; be diligent to obtain the same within your Heart,

# Here JESUS teaches in the Heart.
## VIII. Figure. <note>This "Figure" refers to an accompanying emblem or illustration common in 17th-century devotional books, depicting Jesus acting upon a human heart.</note>

Whoever desires the greatest Teacher
To receive his teaching in their heart;
Let them keep themselves quiet <note>The Dutch "stil" implies a meditative, internal silence, a key practice in Pietism to hear the "inner voice" of God.</note> within themselves,
And pay heed to Jesus’ paths of love;
Let them withdraw themselves from the world,
From high and deep earthly matters,
And listen to Him who gave such lessons,
Which the world does not appreciate. <note>Original: "smaaken," literally "to taste." It suggests that worldly people have no appetite for spiritual truths.</note>

Thus shall that heart, taught by God,
Be more learned than the wise;
Whose deep intellect is turned away
From that which Jesus comes to praise.
They shall understand that beautiful language,
Which many wise men do not grasp,
Because they disdain Jesus’ sweet welcome
And let him pass them by.

Happy is that heart, wherein
The most beautiful Teacher comes to teach;
How clearly one then understands His meaning,
When Jesus comes to dwell within us.
How vain, futile—oh, how small—
Is all wisdom of the senses,
Compared to that Sunshine;
That Heavenly light, 그hat Ray of love.

Come, most beautiful Sun of Souls,
Let your lessons echo in my heart;
And let me from your Blessed spring,
Drink steadily of Heavenly wisdom.

->**1 CORINTHIANS 1: 20.**<-

> *inquirer of this age? Has God not made the wisdom of this*
> *world foolish?*

->**1 CORINTHIANS 2: 12.**<-

> *But we have not received the spirit of the world, but*
> *the spirit which is from God, so that we might know the things*
> *that are given to us by God.*

<meta>This page marks the beginning of section VIII in the "Heart-Theology" series. The previous page concluded with a scripture from 1 Corinthians regarding the limitations of worldly wisdom. This page introduces a new emblem and poem focusing on Jesus as the internal "Teacher" of the soul, continuing the theme that true spiritual understanding comes from divine intervention rather than human scholarship.</meta>

# Heart-Theology.

## VIII.

### Here Jesus Teaches in the Heart.

<image-desc>An emblem engraving showing a large heart suspended in the clouds. Inside the heart, the Christ Child sits at a desk as a teacher, pointing to an open book that reads "They shall all be taught by God" <note>Original Latin: "Erunt docti omnes a Deo."</note>. Rays of light emanate from his head. Above the heart, a celestial light pours down through a funnel-like opening into the top of the heart. Below the heart, two angels are kneeling in prayer on the clouds, looking upward.</image-desc>

Whoever teaches, whoever preaches;
It cannot compare to what Jesus speaks.
Therefore, my faithful Savior <term>Heyland: a traditional Dutch term for Savior, literally "Healer" or "One who brings holiness."</term>,
Please be my Teacher <note>Original: "Leeraar." In this context, it refers to Christ as the "Internal Word" who instructs the believer's conscience.</note> always.
Impress your word into my soul with power:
And grant also that it may be fulfilled.

### VIII.

## Dialogue of the Soul.

1. **W**hat do I see here again, O good Jesus? Are you not sitting here in the Heart as a Teacher? Yes, surely you must come into the Heart and teach there; for if you do not open the Heart, then all the teaching of Men is in vain, and if you yourself do not enlighten it with your light.

2. Be then also in my Heart my Teacher, O only true Master Teacher. Enlighten for me the eyes of my understanding, and open my Heart for me; radiate upon it with the light of your grace, and let the gaze of your Love descend into it. Grant this so that, alongside the outward reading and hearing of the Holy Scripture, I may find the enlightenment of your grace and Love within me—that my Soul may thus be illumined by your light and kindled by your Love, and in true, living experience <term>bevindelykheyd: a central term in Dutch pietism referring to "experiential" or "experimental" faith—knowledge of God that is felt and lived in the heart rather than just understood intellectually.</term> learn to know you and herself.

3. O Jesus! You, the only and intimate Teacher of my heart, accept me from this hour and moment into your School. Teach me inwardly, so that my Heart may perceive it, unto a life and comfort for my Soul.

<meta>catchword: 4. Stands</meta>

# Theology of the Heart.

4. Arise then, my Soul; follow after this intimate and only Teacher; where indeed in Heaven or on Earth would you find a better Teacher than Jesus himself, the Mouth of Truth? He shall teach you inwardly and make it pleasing to you, of which other Saints <note>original: "Heylige"</note> have testified outwardly: others may fill the brains of men with words and some knowledge, but this One shall fill your heart with truth and the essence itself; others make men learned in Scripture or the world, but this One makes you more Pious <note>original: "Vromer." In 17th-century Dutch, this implies not just religious observance, but a deep, lived-out godliness and integrity.</note>; others speak to your ears, but this One speaks into your Heart.

### Sighing of the Soul.
<note>A "sighing" (zuchtinge) in this tradition is a short, rhythmic prayer or a brief aspiration of the soul toward God.</note>

> *Why should I not turn my heart to you,*
> *O Jesus, faithful Master, for that which you command;*
> *And that which pleases you, to be allowed to learn from you,*
> *Since I also simultaneously enjoy your faithful help.*

God is meek, for the Meek hear God’s voice, which the hasty and angry people do not hear; for when the wind storms, and the windows and doors creak, then one cannot hear well.

If you would now hear that fatherly, hidden Heavenly Word within you, which is spoken in the holiest, innermost ground, then all haste and turbulence must settle within you, and you must be a meek little sheep, and know your great failings, and leave them, and listen to this voice with quiet meekness; this is hidden from all those who are not sheep.

When Jesus speaks, everything is silent,

# Heart-Theology.

### Closer Observation.

O Divine and faithful Savior <term>Savior: Dutch *Saligmaker*, literally "one who makes blessed" or "bringer of salvation"</term> Jesus, You are the light of the world, illuminating every person coming into the world: You are the fountain of life, in whose light we behold the light. O Lord! when it is Your pleasure to teach and instruct the human heart, then all other Teachers must stand outside, because the Soul then begins to hear what You speak in the heart.

Who can express that joy, when a heart sighing for You and longing for You becomes aware that You wish to teach her, and that she hears You speaking in her Soul? Then it is nothing other than as if Heaven has come to earth.

And especially then, O most beloved Jesus! when You make the Soul understand Your Divine word in the innermost depths of her heart, then she must cry out: *Truly the Lord is in this place* <note>A reference to Genesis 28:16, where Jacob realizes God is present even in a lonely place.</note>, because she begins to see something with faithful eyes that she previously read and studied, but nonetheless could not understand; therefore You say, O sweetest Jesus! I shall send you the Spirit of truth, and He shall lead you into the truth.

->JOHN 15: 7.<- <note>The original OCR reads "Joh. 5: 7," but the text cited is clearly John 15:7; this is likely a historical printing error.</note>

> *If you remain in me, and my words remain in*
> *you, whatever you wish, you shall desire, and*
> *it shall be done for you.*

# Living Wisdom

The wisdom that a person does not learn from Christ,
May nevermore remain steadfast before God;
Therefore, he who aims for and rightly desires the wisdom of God,
Must turn himself to that Fountain, who wishes to serve <term>serve: Dutch *geryven*, meaning to accommodate, satisfy, or provide for the needs of others</term> everyone.

> O Jesus! do come into my learning,
> And draw my Heart constantly on high,
> So that I may converse with You,
> And have You steadily before my eyes.
> If You teach me by Your spirit from within;
> Then I shall be wise according to Your will;
> This is the highest that I can gain;
> O Jesus, teach me but in silence:
> In silence only can one find You,
> Stripped of all earthly noise.
> There You give to Your most beloved;
> The blessed goods of Your House.
> In silence the Soul sits down;
> The Heart fixed on things above,
> With the most beautiful Jesus in view;
> Who gives His love back to the Soul.
> There love shines in the Heart,
> That love without measure or ground <note>original: "sonder peyl of grond," a common mystical expression referring to the infinite depth of divine love</note>,
> Which the sweetest Jesus bestows;
> On those who long with much pain.

<meta>This page continues the "Heart-Theology" series, moving from the general prayer of the previous page's song to the specific imagery of the "IX. Figure." ### Here JESUS writes in the Heart.
## IX. Figure.

What do You not do, O Jesus! my beloved,
In the Heart of mine, where You are indwelling;
What do You not do, O truest friend of friends,
To the poorest man who suffers before Your countenance?
Is it not enough that You come to teach the heart,
And dwell therein constantly with Your spirit?
But You still want to turn the Soul toward things,
That serve her salvation most of all.
You want the heart to be Your own,
Completely and entirely, in what it does and thinks;
So that Your name may be praised as Holy,
By those to whom You grant such things.
Yes, dear Jesus, freely imprint on my heart,
With Your brush <note>original: "penseel"; this suggests Jesus is painting or writing on the heart like an artist, a common motif in 17th-century Dutch emblem books where the heart is a canvas for the divine.</note>, Your Divine spirit,
Freely imprint therein, though it might even pain the flesh,
All that which You judge to be the best?
Yes, imprint therein, my final dying times,
How this flesh must go to the dismal grave;
How daily my years glide away;
How time cuts off the thread of my life.
Yes, imprint therein the great day of days,
When You, exalted on Your high Throne,
Shall present Your judgment to all flesh,
And judge them like the Son of man.
Yes, imprint therein the miseries of the wicked,
That eternal woe, and mournful, dismal sound;
Because they did not know You as their King,
So You cast them out from You forever.
Yes, for my comfort, in all my opposition,
Imprint on my Soul, that greatest joy;
Which You shall one day give to Your children,
After this time, this still gladdens my Soul.

->DAN. 12: 2.<-

> *And many of those who sleep in the dust of the earth shall awaken, some to everlasting life, and others to shame and everlasting abhorrence.*

<term>Son of man</term> <note>A title used by Jesus to refer to himself, emphasizing both his humanity and his role as the divine judge at the end of time.</note>

<keywords>Jesus Christ, Heart-Theology, memento mori, Last Judgment, eternal life, Daniel 12:2, spiritual discipline, Dutch Pietism</keywords>

### IX.
## *Dialogue of the Soul.*

1. Oh dear Lord, in this Image it is set before my eyes that it is necessary that You, with the brush of Your Holy Spirit, work upon my Heart with all kinds of good and holy thoughts and stirrings, so that by these means all unclean and unauthorized thoughts and stirrings might be kept out of my heart. <note>The author uses the metaphor of a "brush" (penceel) to suggest that the Holy Spirit "paints" or crafts the interior life of the believer like a masterpiece.</note>

2. For in order for all unclean images and thoughts to be driven out of my heart, You, O good Jesus, must do that very thing Yourself; this occurs through Your goodness when You pour good thoughts into my Heart through Your good Spirit.

3. Now, my Soul, turn yourself then to your Savior <term>Saligmaker|Literally "Blessed-maker," a common Dutch term for Christ as the one who grants salvation and eternal happiness</term>; recognize Him for who He truly is: your Redeemer and the Giver of all good things. And as often as harmful thoughts, unclean images, or strange fantasies occur to you, turn immediately to your Savior, sighing to Him that He might drive away these evil things by imparting <note>original: "mededeelen"; here meaning the divine act of sharing or bestowing grace and holy impulses.</note> good thoughts to you.

# Heart-Theology.

4. Then, O gracious Lord, drive out from my Heart all unauthorized images and thoughts that displease You, which so often disturb me; for since doing such a thing belongs to You alone, I turn only to You with unspeakable sighing; Ah! see how my heart is often tormented by such fantasies; Ah Lord! arise and still the raging of this stormy sea within me. <note>The "stormy sea" is a frequent biblical metaphor for a restless or troubled mind, referencing Christ calming the storm in the Gospels.</note>

## Aspirations of the Soul.
<note>original: "Suchtinge der Ziele." In 17th-century devotional literature, a "sighing" (suchtinge) refers to a short, intense prayer or an "aspiration" of the soul toward God.</note>

> If You, O Lord, with Your Brush,
> Paint my Heart more and more,
> After You have first removed the filthy ground <term>ground|In painting, the "ground" is the base layer applied to a canvas. The author suggests God must first scrub away the "filth" of sin before He can paint a holy image.</term>
> According to Your covenant:
> Then I shall draw joy and light,
> Through the good work established in me.
> 
> O Jesus, depict within the Heart
> Sin, death, the Hellish grave,
> Your judgment in righteousness,
> And the blessed joy after this time, <note>A reference to the eternal life that follows earthly existence.</note>
> So that before Your face,
> I may direct my conduct toward virtue;
> O Son of God! O dear friend,
> Embrace me as Your bride and child. <note>The "Bride" imagery reflects the "Mystical Marriage," a common theme where the individual soul is viewed as the spouse of Christ.</note>

# Living Images

> O Lord, please take Your brush, <term>brush|original: "Penceel." This continues the metaphor from the previous page where Jesus is depicted as an artist painting holy virtues onto the "canvas" of the human soul.</term>
> Fill my heart entirely,
> And imprint there Your divine image,
> Sharing Your holiness with me,
> So that no idle fantasy,
> No worldly yoke nor slavery,
> May find a place or room in me
> Any longer, to cause such a failing.

---

> Shape our Heart, all-wise Lord,
> With images that teach us what is good,
> Which lead us away from the creature, <note>In 17th-century theology, "the creature" (het schepsel) refers to anything created—the material world and its distractions—as opposed to the Creator.</note>
> To Him who gave the creature its being.
> No place there may remain empty,
> Where Satan might drive his subtleties in;
> God’s judgment follows, death, and the grave,
> Which rewards the good and punishes the evil.

---

> You who are exalted in high status
> Below in this earthly life,
> You who carry scepter and crown,
> You who sit upon a golden throne.
> Happy would you be above all others,
> If you might well please God—
> If, alongside your status and wealth,
> You always had Jesus in your mind;
> So that He might work within your soul,
> Many thousands of good works;
> But oh, alas! stripped of that,
> You are dead in sin. <note>This is a "memento mori" reminder that earthly power and "golden thrones" provide no spiritual safety; true life is found only through internal grace.</note>

<meta>catchword: After-</meta>

<summary>This page concludes the poetic prayer asking God to paint His image on the heart and warns that earthly status is meaningless without the indwelling of Christ. It emphasizes that a heart left "empty" of divine images will inevitably be filled by the influence of Satan.</summary>

# Heart Theology.

## Figure IX.
## Further Reflection.

### DEATH.

The poor weak human, of little value;
Taken from the dust, must return to the earth again:
Ah, that we would daily think of our end,
Then our heart would be more turned toward the Lord.

->*Psalm 90: 3.*<-
->*You turn man back to destruction: and*<-
->*say, return, you children of men.*<- <note>The Dutch word "verbryselinge" implies being crushed back into dust or powder, emphasizing the physical decay of the body.</note>

### THE LAST JUDGMENT.

O day of terror for the wicked, O day of comfort for the pious,
The one to Hell, the other taken up:
Alas! What sad words (are these for the evil soul,
What joy for God’s people,) which their Judge speaks here.

->*2 Cor. 5: 10.*<-
->*For we must all be made manifest before the judgment*<-
->*seat of Christ.*<-

### HEAVENLY GLORY.

O blessed glory! Who can proclaim your joy,
To be with Jesus always, in the praising of Psalms:
To see that Lord face to face!
Who, out of His free love and favor, looked down upon us.

->*Matth. 25: 34.*<-
->*Come, you blessed of my Father, inherit the kingdom that*<-
->*is prepared for you from the foundation of the world.*<-

### HELLISH TERROR.

The sad, gruesome wailing shall last here eternally,
The gnashing of teeth, and the most pitiful mourning:
And one does not die through death, <note>This refers to the "Second Death" mentioned in Revelation—a state of eternal spiritual death where the soul exists in torment without the release of non-existence.</note> oh, sadly eternal,
What a terror you are to the souls of many.

->*Matth. 25: 41.*<-
->*Depart from me, you cursed, into the eternal fire, which*<-
->*is prepared for the Devil and his angels.*<-

## IX.

The Creator of the Universe has sketched before human
->eyes,<-
His goodness, His wisdom, and His power;
Whenever one but casts an eye upon all that He
Has created, it is the most beautiful painting. <note>The Dutch word "schildery" refers to a painting or depiction. In this period, nature was often called "the second book of God," where His divinity could be read like text or seen like art.</note>
Observe the entire circle of the world in all its parts,
See the clouds, sun, and moon, and stars; look below;
Throughout the whole Earth; yes, see the sea as well,
So you must say: it is the most beautiful painting.
Behold man, created so uniquely by God,
With such a quick understanding and wisdom; O, what a wonder!
His body is most beautiful; where is there one like him?
The most beautiful Image of God, and the rarest painting.
All that is in the world, before human eyes on Earth,
Of things that happen, whether of small or great value:
All that one does or works at, whether one is sad or happy,
It depicts nothing to our understanding but a painting.

---

> *portrayed* <note>The original Dutch uses "geschilderd," meaning "painted" or "depicted," echoing the theme of the poem.</note> *among you as crucified.*

## X. Figure.

### Here the Cross of JESUS enters the Heart.

Good Jesus, store the rods <note>original: "roeden." These refer to the bundles of switches used in the Scourging at the Pillar.</note>,
Which made your shoulders bleed;
Crown and Reed-staff, Spear and Gall,
Cords, Nails, Cross and all:
All your suffering, all your pain,
Store that deep within our heart,
That it may taste, and that it may know
What you suffered for our sins:
That it may learn deep humility <note>original: "ootmoet." A central term in Dutch devotional literature, "ootmoed" implies a profound, self-abasing lowliness before God.</note>,
By contemplating how you, Lord,
Great God of eternity,
Became man in place and time,
And have given yourself,
Into death for man's life,
Holy suffering, Holy Cross,
Never depart from our House <note>In the "Heart-Theology" tradition, the heart is frequently depicted as a house or a small room where Christ dwells.</note>.
Make us learn humility through this,
Make us turn ourselves to you;
Let your exceeding love,
Of your Cross and of your death,
Work so constantly in us,
And make us truly realize,
That you, all-sufficient Lord,
Love and cherish sinners tenderly.

->1 CORINTHIANS 1: 23.<-

> *But we preach Christ crucified, to the Jews*
> *certainly a stumbling block, and to the Greeks foolishness.*
